# Migration plan: cockpit to oculus

Execution ledger for `tools/oculus/DESIGN.md`. One bullet is one package of work and one agent dispatch, except the bookkeeping packages the orchestrator does itself (marked). A package is ticked only after its verification commands ran and the orchestrator read the whole diff; the commands are in `orchestrator-state.md`, and nothing is ticked on inspection. The green checkmark is this file's status vocabulary by Axel's instruction of 2026-09-10; everything else in this folder is ASCII. Read `orchestrator-state.md` first after any context compaction. The lettering C, R, S, O is the ledger's own; the cockpit roadmap's letters A to I map onto the O packages as noted.

## Packages, in execution order

One line per package: the status mark, the id, the title, and where the rest is (the brief, the review, the landing note in `orchestrator-state.md`). Lean by Axel's rule of 2026-09-12; nothing about a package lives only here.

- ✅ O41 motion for the actions the reader waits on, the rail's number as the sum of its page's counts, and the clone finding that counts worktrees: runs beside O40, approved by Axel 2026-10-17 (ruling 132)
  - Step 0, motion: an action the reader started and waits on shows motion where it was started, a turning glyph on its button or item or a moving progress line on its view, with words naming what runs, and none under the system's reduce-motion setting: git commit, push, pull, fetch, sync, clone, checkout, merge and stash (a progress line on the Source Control view and the repository's row, the sync glyph turning in the status bar's git item for push, pull, fetch and sync, the words naming the action in place of "busy"); the Search view's search and the Literature search; Submit Job; a Usage refresh the reader asks for; an update task or a scheduled run started by hand. Background work gets no motion: git auto-fetch, the HPC poll, the feed watcher, language servers starting, a file save. The LaTeX build and an app's progress keep the motion they have.
  - Step 0, the rail's number: a view's count is one of three: none, shown on its tab only, or shown on its tab and given to the rail. A rail button shows the sum of the rail counts of its page's tabs, its hover words list each part ("Projects 20, Coauthor 2, Personal 3"), and it takes the most urgent tone among its parts; a tab with a mark and no count gives nothing to the sum and its mark shows on the button when the sum is 0. Rail counts: Projects (in progress), Coauthor (in progress), Personal (building), Inbox, Repositories, Problems, Funding, Applications, Education, Joint Work, Tasks, Audit, Findings (new, the number of findings) and the Session list (new, the sessions waiting for the reader). Tab only: Leads, Processes (new, the programs still running). Nothing to the rail: Archive, Software, Calendar, Radar, each tab keeping what it shows. The tabs keep their own numbers as they are.
  - Step 0, the clone finding: `findings.manifests.cloned_twice` counts a repository's linked worktrees as clones (`placeClones`, `src/lib/data/sources/clones.ts`, compares origin URLs and a worktree reads its main checkout's config); clones are counted by the git folder they share, so a worktree belongs to its main checkout and is never a second clone, and every other finding or list that reads repositories is checked for the same.

On macOS that cannot be done: Electron reads `registerAccelerator` on Linux and Windows alone (`node_modules/electron/electron.d.ts:22562`, `@platform linux,win32`, Electron 44.5.1), so on macOS an item that shows a key also takes it, as the Edit items already do (`src/desktop/windows/menu.ts:6-8`). Electron hands a key to the page first and runs the menu item only when the page did not take it; that order is not verified here. Options: (a) on macOS an item whose key holds Cmd or Ctrl shows the key and takes it when the page leaves it, the press running the same page command once; an item whose key holds neither (`?` for Keyboard Shortcuts, and F5, Shift+F5, F9, F10, F11 and Shift+F11 of the Run menu) shows no key on macOS, since the menu would take it from a text field; those keys join ACCELERATORS (`src/lib/data/helpers/keys/oculus/desktop-keys.ts`), so the apps' keybinding audit counts them as keys oculus takes; off macOS every item shows its key and takes none. Pro: the menu shows the keys on the platform the app is built for. Con: a key the page leaves (Ctrl+Tab while a text field holds the keyboard, which today does nothing) then runs the menu's command; the order of page and menu has to hold, checked in the app at the look. (b) on macOS the items show no key, the page keeping every key; off macOS they show it and take none; ACCELERATORS unchanged; Help > Keyboard Shortcuts lists the keys. Pro: exactly the behaviour ruled, nothing unverified. Con: no key beside any item on macOS. Recommendation: (a), since the menu is meant to show the keys and the page keeps first claim on each; (b) is the lazy option. Ruled 2026-10-18, see the ruling line of 147; /spec again.

- Z934 (2026-10-19, O38's /spec, the orchestrator, automode): a file check of Recover keeps its result in `~/.oculus/recover/<hub>/<key>.checks.json`, one row per file with its size, mtime and the checksum it matched; a row whose size or mtime no longer matches the file is stale, and the file then reads present (self-rule).
- Z935 (2026-10-19, O38's /spec, the orchestrator, automode): a Recover source may carry `strip: <n>`, the number of leading folders the unpack drops, and ptb-xl's has `strip: 1`; an `archive: true` zip that `SHA256SUMS.txt` does not list is checked by its size alone (self-rule).
- Z936 (2026-10-19, O38's /spec, the orchestrator, automode): a hub that holds a `.oculus/data/` folder reports a missing default collection file as a finding, as it does a named one, so dropping the hub's `collections:` block loses no finding (self-rule).
- Z932 (2026-10-17, O40's /spec, the orchestrator, automode): the close question over unsaved text reads "Do you want to save the changes to this tab before it closes?" with Don't Save, Cancel and Save, the keyboard on Save; the add's undo row reads "add undone"; the Refactor Preview after a drag is held to 1 s (self-rule).
- Z931 (2026-10-17, O40's /spec, the orchestrator, automode): row 24 of `agents/reviews/O37-defects.md` needs no change; the read at a folder's right-press decides Open or New Presentation (`picks.svelte.ts:238-247`) (self-rule).
- Z930 (2026-10-17, O37's landing, the orchestrator, automode): `npm run test:quick` holds 45 s over a change of a builder's size (20.8 s); over a whole step's change it selects nearly the whole unit suite (92.9 s), which the landing list runs; making that fit is not O37's part; calling the whole-step run green was the lazy option, not taken (self-rule).

## Blocked, and by what

- O16: no longer blocked. The `Notification` and `Stop` hooks exist since 2026-09-10 in the hub's and cathedra's local settings, written by Axel.
- O30: decided after O19 from experience, by design.
- O38: does not converge (open item 158, 2026-10-19): its fourth /spec ran the brief check, which left three choices for Axel: PhysioNet credentials, the Synapse token and the project-cleanup skill's characters. /spec refuses O38 until a ruling rewrites it. O39: O38's landing (ruling 122).
